{"year": 2023, "dataset": "acs/acs5", "table_code": "B01003", "geo_for": "county:*", "geo_in": "state:06"}\n    - {"year": 2023, "dataset": "acs/acs5/subject", "table_code": "S0101", "geo_for": "state:*", "use_groups": true}\n    - {"year": 2023, "dataset": "acs/acs5", "table_code": "B01001", "geo_for": {"state (or part)": "*"}, "geo_in": {"metropolitan statistical area/micropolitan statistical area": "35620"}}\n    \nresolve_area_name - \n    Resolve a friendly geography name to its Census code.\n    \n    Use this when you need to convert a single area name to its FIPS code.\n    \n    Input must be valid JSON with these fields:\n    - name: Area name (required)\n    - geography_type: Geography level (default: "state")\n    - dataset: Census dataset (default: "acs/acs5")\n    - year: Year (default: 2023)\n    - parent: Parent geography dict (optional)\n    \n    Examples:\n    - {"name": "California", "geography_type": "state"}\n    - {"name": "Los Angeles County", "geography_type": "county", "parent": {"state": "06"}}\n    \ncreate_chart - \n    Create data visualizations from census data\n    \n    Supports both single-series and multi-series charts (auto-detected).\n    Multi-series charts automatically group by geography when multiple areas are present.\n    \n    Input must be valid JSON with these fields:\n    - color_column: Optional column name for multi-series grouping (auto-detected if not provided)\n    - data: Census data dict from census_api_call tool\n    \ngeography_hierarchy - \n    Look up geography hierarchy ordering for a dataset/year/for_level.\n\n    Input must be valid JSON with:\n    - action: "get_hierarchy_ordering" (default)\n    - dataset: Dataset path (e.g., "acs/acs5")\n    - year: Census year (e.g., 2023)\n    - for_level: Target geography token (e.g., "county", "place")\n    - action: "validate_variables" (default) or "list_variables"\n    - dataset: Dataset path like "acs/acs5" or "acs/acs5/subject"\n    - year: Census year (e.g., 2023)\n    - variables: Array of variable codes to validate (required for validate_variables)\n    -
